# Correction ledger — frequency-weighted reinforcement

Design per Kam (2026-08-03, voice): *"similar to when people learn, frequency is
used for reinforcement — if a mistake happens once, that could be isolated; if it
happens more than once, an increased weight is given."*

## The weight scale

Weight = number of occurrences. Each recurrence escalates the response:

| w | Meaning | Required response |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Isolated** — could be noise | Log it. Write a lesson file only if it would change future behaviour (importance filter). |
| 2 | **Reinforced** — a pattern, not noise | Lesson file mandatory. If one existed and didn't fire, diagnose *why* (wrong file? too abstract? bad retrieval handle?) and fix the lesson, not just the mistake. |
| ≥3 | **Regression** — the system is failing to learn | Treat like a failing test (no-skip rule). Automatic promotion candidate: move the rule into `identity/` or into *enforcement* (launcher check, hook, CLAUDE.md line). Raise it with Kam at the next briefing. |

Rules:
- Same *underlying* mistake in a new costume still increments the weight — match
  on root cause, not surface form.
- Weights never decay automatically. They are only retired at weekly
  consolidation, with the reasoning written in the audit note.
- Positive reinforcement counts too: when Kam explicitly praises a behaviour,
  log it as `praise` — repetition there tells us what to *keep* doing.

| 2026-09-09 | 🔴 **A HEALTH CHECK PASSED ON A JOB THAT HAD NEVER EXECUTED, BECAUSE IT KEYED ON AN ARTEFACT WEDNESDAY'S OWN HANDS WERE KEEPING FRESH — the checker was the thing defeating the check.** Building `chat_sync` on Kam's instruction (one page, both agents, no agent intervention), the launchd job read as healthy on every instrument a reader would reach for: `plutil -lint` OK · `launchctl list` showed it loaded · the script ran perfectly by hand · and `doctor.sh` printed **✓ chat_sync loaded AND firing**. **It had run ZERO times.** `launchctl print` said `last exit code = 78: EX_CONFIG` and the log had gained nothing in two minutes. **THE CAUSE, measured against the three plists that DO work on this machine: `StandardOutPath`/`StandardErrorPath` pointed at `/Volumes/DevMASTER/…`, and launchd cannot open those on an external volume — so it refuses the ENTIRE job, never runs the program, and emits nothing.** nassync/wake/close all use `~/Library/Logs`; moving them there gave `last exit code = 0`. **The program path on `/Volumes` is fine — it is only the log paths, which is why it looks like a working plist.** 🔴 **THE HALF THAT IS THE LESSON: the doctor check was written to warn if the log had not moved in 10 minutes, and it passed because WEDNESDAY WAS RUNNING THE SCRIPT BY HAND WHILE TESTING IT.** The check could not distinguish launchd's runs from the author's — the only discrimination it existed to make. **This is a NEW costume of [[2026-08-07_a-check-that-cannot-fail]]: not a check that measures the wrong property, but one whose EVIDENCE THE TESTER IS ACTIVELY MANUFACTURING while testing. Exercising a mechanism and monitoring it are the same activity here, and the exercise forges the monitor's evidence.** Re-keyed to `launchctl print`'s own `last exit code`, which nothing a seat does by hand can move; **red-proofed on four stubbed values — `0` and `(never exited)` pass, `78: EX_CONFIG` and `127` warn.** **AND THE INTERVAL PROOF WAS BUILT TO THE SAME STANDARD after `runs` alone nearly fooled it once** (it incremented on a *reload*, not a timer fire): the final proof required **`runs` AND the log's newest timestamp to move together** — 1→2 with 18:01:05→18:02:06 — because either instrument alone is consistent with the wrong story. | correction | +1 (a-check-that-cannot-fail; the EVIDENCE-MANUFACTURED-BY-THE-TESTER costume — NEW) · +1 (a launchd job dead-on-arrival with every conventional instrument green) | [[2026-08-07_a-check-that-cannot-fail]] (the parent — ask what would make it fail; here the answer was "nothing, while I am testing it") · [[2026-08-06_artifact-presence-is-not-execution]] (the escalation ladder: loaded is rung 2, `runs` incrementing on a reload is rung 4, `runs` AND a log line together is rung 6) · [[2026-09-08_a-false-absence-is-usually-my-own-instrument]] (rules 11-12: a control must fail INDEPENDENTLY — the log did not) · [[2026-08-06_exercise-mechanisms-before-arming]] (four branches exercised, and the arming step still shipped dead) · [[2026-08-25_travel-drive-stale-pointers]] (the external-volume half) | **FIXED and PROVEN before it was reported: plist logs moved off the volume, doctor re-keyed and red-proofed both directions, interval fire confirmed on two independent instruments. | 2026-09-09 | 🟡 **WEDNESDAY MADE A STATE CHANGE IN ANOTHER PROJECT'S REPO — retargeting two PRs — ON KAM'S EXPLICIT WORD, AND DISCLOSED IT AS AN EXCEPTION RATHER THAN ABSORBING IT.** After the merge incident, a sweep of all 42 open PRs found **3 not based on `develop` and 2 ARMED the same way #915 had been** — their base branch already fully contained in develop (#916 `ahead_by:0 behind:2`; **#879 `ahead_by:0 behind:95`, armed a long time and unnoticed**). #900 was `ahead_by:4`, a genuine live stack, correctly left. **Control: KS-926's branch read `ahead_by:1`, so the check discriminates.** Wednesday reported it and said it would not act — *"that is a write into their repository under their identity and I have kept off that all morning."* **Kam replied: *"Can you address these yourself, or do you need me to do it? You've got my approval."*** **THE TENSION IS REAL AND WAS NOT RESOLVED BY PICKING THE EASY SIDE: the 2026-08-03 read-only tracker grant says in terms *"no state changes... writes remain with each project's own agent under its own identity"*, and a PR base change is a state change.** Against that: he asked directly, pre-approved the answer, the action is reversible, and launching a whole agent session to change two dropdown values is disproportionate to the boundary it protects. **Done, and DISCLOSED to him as a scoped exception with the alternative offered back — *"if you would rather I had launched an agent to do it instead, say so and that is what I will do next time"* — rather than letting the precedent set itself quietly.** **Verified by RE-READING both from the API rather than trusting the PATCH response: #916's head `584b12ba1eed` matches exactly what s158 reported pushing, so the retarget moved no code.** Re-swept after: 1 off-develop remaining, the live stack. | grant/exception | 1 (NEW: a standing read-only boundary crossed on the principal's explicit direction — recorded as an exception, not folded into the rule) | [[2026-08-03_grant-readonly-tracker-access]] (**the rule crossed — its hard limit 1 is *READ-ONLY, ever***) · [[2026-07-31_manage-dont-do]] (writes stay with the project's agent) · [[2026-08-07_protocol-v1.3-signed-delegation]] (**a grant is narrowed or widened by HIM; this was him widening it for two objects, and it is recorded so the next seat does not read it as general**) · [[2026-08-16_classification-is-the-field-that-grants-authority]] (*reversible* was the scope word, and it was established by the fact the change is a dropdown that can be set back) | **THE SCOPE, written down so a successor cannot inherit it as licence: this covers TWO NAMED PRs on 2026-09-09 and nothing else. It is not a standing relaxation of the read-only tracker grant, and the next write into a client repo stops for Kam exactly as before.** **AND THE FINDING UNDER IT IS WORTH MORE THAN THE FIX: #879 had been armed for 95 commits.** Nobody was looking, because a PR with a healthy `mergeable_state` and a live base branch shows no symptom until the moment it is merged. **Standing line added to `brief-standing-lines.md`: before merging any PR, confirm its base is not already contained in the target.** |

| 2026-09-08 | 🟠 **WEDNESDAY RAN A WRITE-ONLY TOOL WITH `--help` AND POSTED THE WORD TO KAM'S READING SURFACE — he read it, thought Wednesday was in trouble, and asked what was wrong.** Probing `chat_reply.sh --help` for its usage, the script has NO usage guard: its only mode is to append its argument to the panel. So `--help` went up as a message from Wednesday, the panel's autoplay read it aloud, and Kam replied *"I got a message from you saying help. What do you need?"* and then *"I got a little bit worried. I thought you needed help."* **The root cause is a habit, not a typo: `--help` is treated as universally safe, and for a tool whose ONLY action is to write it is a live invocation.** **The attempted REPAIR was the more interesting half and it was refused, correctly.** Wednesday edited the entry's text in place to a correction note, keeping the original in a `superseded_text` field — a quarantine, not a delete. `chat_streams.py` then REFUSED to rebuild: its orphan detector keys on `(ts, text)`, so an entry whose text was CORRECTED in the stream reads as an entry the rebuild would DELETE. **The guard was right in spirit and its key is one field too strict.** Wednesday reverted the edit rather than reaching for `--harvest` (which would have re-filed `--help` as a fresh message) or weakening the guard — the correction went to Kam as a new message instead, which is what it should have been from the start: **do not rewrite history on the principal's surface; append.** | correction | 1 (NEW: a write-only tool probed with `--help`) · +1 (chat_streams orphan guard: a CORRECTED entry is indistinguishable from a LOST one) | [[2026-08-15_a-gui-open-is-a-write]] (an action classified as reading that writes — the same shape, pointed at a CLI flag) · [[2026-08-26_never-delete-cleanup-means-quarantine]] (the repair honoured it; the guard still refused) · [[2026-09-07_a-prior-ruling-gate-refusal-is-a-research-prompt]] (a refusal is a question — answered by reverting, not by overriding) · [[2026-08-20_dashboard-shows-only-live-work]] | **RULE: before passing any flag to a tool in `2_Project_Files/tools/`, read its arg parser — a tool with no usage guard has no safe probe.** Corrected to Kam in the same minutes, leading with it. | 2026-09-08 | 🟡 **A POINTER TAP FAILED SILENTLY BECAUSE WEDNESDAY PASSED A PANE ID WHERE THE TOOL WANTS A PANE NAME — and the first run's rc was never read, so it looked like nothing happened at all.** `cockpit.sh say %171 …` returned rc 1 with no visible output; the pane was unchanged and the agent kept waiting. **`say` resolves its inbox from `inbox_routing.conf`, which is keyed on the pane NAME (`Secuura/Blockchain`), so `grep -F '%171|'` matched nothing and `die` fired.** Diagnosed by re-running with the rc captured on its own line and then `bash -x` — not by re-reading the script, which is where the eye goes and where the defect was not. **This is the runtime-id lesson in a new costume: that file says a handover records a mechanism by its PATH, never by a pane id — and here Wednesday fed a pane id to the mechanism ITSELF.** A pane id is correct for `tmux capture-pane` and wrong for `cockpit.sh say`, which is exactly the kind of adjacency that makes it easy. **Zero cost — the mail was already verified at the destination, so nothing false was delivered anywhere; the agent simply waited ~4 minutes longer.** | correction | 1 (NEW: a runtime pane id passed to a NAME-keyed tool) · +1 (never-discard-stderr: the first invocation's rc was not captured) | [[2026-09-07_a-mechanism-is-recorded-by-its-path-not-its-runtime-id]] (the parent — recorded here as its INVOCATION half) · [[2026-08-06_never-discard-stderr]] (rc on its own line is what turned an invisible failure into a one-minute diagnosis) · [[2026-08-26_zsh-has-no-pipestatus]] (`| tail` also ate the `die` message) · [[2026-09-01_a-tap-is-a-pointer-not-a-message]] | FIXED: re-tapped with `"Secuura/Blockchain"`; the `--mail` freshness gate verified the mail at 04:41:56Z and reported `delivered (prompt clear)`. s150 consumed it and is amending #907. **Standing: read the rc of every tap, and never `tail` a command whose failure message is the thing you need.** |

Caught before shipping by asking what else invokes the thing being disabled; `SPEAK_FROM_SERVER=1` exempts the server's own call and all three branches were exercised (terminal SILENT · server SPEAKS · explicit override SPEAKS). **The rule: before disabling a mechanism, enumerate its CALLERS — a shared implementation behind two user-visible features means turning one off turns both off.** | correction | 1 | [[2026-08-07_enumerate-every-surface-before-done]] · [[2026-09-08_the-check-ran-and-was-not-checking-the-thing]] (its sibling: a change that LOOKS like the feature working) | caught pre-ship; 3 branches proven |

| 2026-09-07 | **A COMMIT SUBJECT BEGINNING WITH `#` IS SILENTLY DISCARDED WHEN THE COMMIT IS COMPLETED BY `rebase --continue` — and the BODY's first line is promoted into its place, so the log looks fine.** Commit `335102d3`'s intended subject was *"#892 NO GO: the fix reproduced its own ticket's defect one level up"*. The log shows *"A green PROVISIONED writes a manifest…"* — the second paragraph. **Nothing errored and nothing warned.** **The precise mechanism, measured rather than assumed:** `git commit -F -` uses cleanup mode `whitespace`, which does NOT strip comment lines — which is why `#890 r2 GO-with-findings` (c1c04e37) and `#889 Finding 2 closed` (849b94c2) kept their subjects earlier the same day. **`rebase --continue` uses the EDITOR path, whose default cleanup DOES strip them.** Proven with a positive control: `git stripspace --strip-comments` on `#test subject\n\nbody line` returns `body line` alone. **So the same message survives one path and is mutilated by the other**, which is why it took a Secuura-style PR number to expose it. **How it was found is the other half:** Wednesday grepped the daily note for a phrase, got **0 hits**, and — instead of concluding the note was lost — **ran a control grep for a phrase it knew was there.** The control returned 1, so the instrument was fine and the search string was wrong. **That control is what turned a false alarm into a real finding**, and it is the same discipline that failed earlier today when `ls` and `head` were used to prove an absence. **The rule: never begin a commit subject with `#`** — put the PR number after a word (`PR #892 NO GO: …`) — **or pass `--cleanup=verbatim`.** **The general form, which is the part worth keeping: a message passed through a tool that INTERPRETS it can lose content with no error, and the loss is invisible because what remains still reads correctly.** Same family as the unquoted heredoc executing backticks and the `-q` flag suppressing the discriminating line. | correction | w+1 (prose destroyed in transit by a tool that interprets it) | [[2026-08-29_unquoted-heredoc-executes-backticks]] · [[2026-08-07_a-check-that-cannot-fail]] (the control that found it) | rule only; `335102d3`'s subject is not recoverable and history was NOT rewritten for a cosmetic loss |

| 2026-09-07 | **WEDNESDAY SPECIFIED A FIX MECHANISM WITHOUT MEASURING WHAT IT DID TO THE PROPERTY IN QUESTION — and the fix had the EXACT flaw Wednesday had just rejected the alternative for.** Ruling #890 round 2, Wednesday told the seat to replace `encodeURIComponent` with `Buffer.from(v,'utf8').toString('base64url')`, asserting it would remove the `URIError` **and** eliminate the `-` sentinel that made the scope join non-injective — and explicitly warned *"do not just add a try/catch: that fixes the 500 and leaves F3 standing."* **Three errors, all found by the seat, all measured:** (1) `base64url('-')` is the constant `LQ`, so the collision survives — **encoding a sentinel RENAMES it, it does not remove it**; (2) a total encoder ALONE trades the 500 for a **fresh collision class**, since every unpaired surrogate encodes to the same bytes (`b64('\ud800') == b64('\ud801') == b64('\ufffd') == "77-9"`) — **a new hole in the very property the PR exists to establish, which is Wednesday's own objection to the try/catch, applied to Wednesday's own fix**; (3) **the two instructions in that same mail contradicted each other** — the wire cell Wednesday specified (`tenantId:"\ud800"` → 400) **cannot pass** under Wednesday's own fix, which returns 200. **The self-check attestation on that mail PASSED and did not catch it, because the check compared ticket ids and numbers, not MECHANISM against SPECIFIED CELL.** Zero cost — the seat measured instead of complying, and the standing line *"if an instruction from Wednesday looks wrong, say so"* is what produced it. **The rule this earns: when a brief names a specific fix MECHANISM (not just a requirement), the mechanism is a claim about the product and needs the same provenance as a count — either measure what it does to the property, or say "a candidate shape, unmeasured — verify before adopting." the fix shape is a per-episode fire-once (reset `fcnt` on fire, or a `fired_$key` marker cleared when the hash changes). **🔴 MECHANISM RETRACTED AND CORRECTED 2026-09-07 20:4x (same day, by the seat that wrote the first half of it), on the FOURTH re-fire:** the account above was composed from the leg's own lines WITHOUT READING LINE 30, which they depend on — **`STATE_DIR="$(mktemp -d ...)"` is a PER-INVOCATION temp dir**, so `cnt`, `fcnt` and the hash do NOT persist across invocations. **What actually happens: the script loops internally (`sleep $INTERVAL` at `:217`) and EVERY fire path is `exit 0` — which kills the invocation and DESTROYS that state.** The runner restarts it, the counters reset to zero, and `STABLE_N` samples later the same condition is re-derived from scratch. **So there IS a reset-after-fire — it is the process exit — and the true defect is sharper than 'no fire-once': THE MECHANISM THAT RESETS THE COUNTER IS THE SAME MECHANISM THAT FIRES THE ALERT, so a fire can never suppress its own repeat.** **This VOIDS the fix shape both rows recorded:** a `fired_$key` marker in `$STATE_DIR` would be wiped by the very exit that wrote it. **The correct shape is the pattern already working in the same file** — a PERSISTENT marker under `$CTX_STATE` (`cockpit/state/`) keyed by pane AND capture hash, cleared when the hash changes, exactly as the ctx legs use `ctx_fired_${gen}_NN`. **Retraction SCOPE, stated so it takes nothing true with it:** both rows' operational conclusions STAND — the alert is true about the render and false about the situation; leg (a) runs first so a repeat cannot mask a real wake; and not editing this file while something depends on it is still right, now better founded because the fix is not the one-liner either row called it. **Handled instead by making the triage CHEAP** — the prescribed order collapsed into one combined command (inbound-only inbox + detector + shell count), rather than three calls per alert. **Wednesday is at 72% and a 75-minute alert storm at 1-2% per triage is a real path into the rotation band on noise alone**, which is the honest reason this is recorded rather than shrugged at.
